<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_011"></a>
## IO-011: Output And

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_011` · Kind **B** · Scope executors with output validation enabled (the default, CONC-026) · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `60bea095195949aa`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| `And(children)` is a node of an output spec that represents a parallel fork in which all children are active.<br><br>*When* a transition whose output spec is `And(children)` fires and its action completes, every child, with nested `And`s flattened, *shall* have received tokens: the produced set contains a place that the child names.<br><br>The same holds for an `And` nested in the alternative that an inner `Xor` chose: the produced set *shall* equal, on the places the output spec names, one output branch of the output spec.<br><br>That output branch is unique, because the output branches of a spec that builds are pairwise distinct sets ([IO-012]).<br><br>This requirement applies to an executor with output validation enabled, which is the default ([CONC-026]).<br><br>*If* a single output branch (one choice at every `Xor`, with nested `And`s flattened) names the same place twice, *then* the output spec *shall* be rejected, with<br><br>*If* the output spec names a place twice in one output branch, *then* `&lt;P>` in the message *shall* be the place that the following walk of the spec reports first, so that every implementation names the same place:<br><br>A `Place(P)` node and a `ForwardInput(from, P)` node name `P`.<br><br>A `Timeout` node walks its child and names the places its child names.<br><br>An `Xor` node walks its children left to right.<br><br>*If* a child reports a place, *then* the `Xor` *shall* report that place.<br><br>Otherwise the `Xor` names the places its children name, each once, in the order the walk first meets them.<br><br>An `And` node walks its children left to right.<br><br>*If* a child reports a place, *then* the `And` *shall* report that place.<br><br>Otherwise, *if* a place the child names, taken in the order the walk first met them, is already named by an earlier child, *then* the `And` *shall* report the first such place.<br><br>Otherwise the `And` names the places its children name, each once, in the order the walk first meets them.<br><br>The walk reports a place exactly when some output branch names a place twice, because the alternatives of an `Xor` under an `And` are chosen independently of the `And`'s other children. | 1. Let Build be the builder (Build t = none: t builds; Build t = some msg: building t fails with msg) and F the firings.<br>2. For every transition t whose output spec o names a place twice in one output branch (DupBranch), building t fails; and if t's input places are distinct, it fails with IO-011's message for t and the place p the walk of o reports (firstDup o = some p, msgDup t p).<br>3. For every transition t that builds (Builds) whose output spec is an And node o with no Timeout node in it:<br>4. for every child c of o (nested Ands flattened) and every deposit d, every firing of t that deposits d produces some place that c names;<br>5. for every deposit d, every firing of t that deposits d has as its produced set, on the places o names, exactly the places of the output branch at one index of o's branch list, and of no branch at any other index (IO_011_uniqueBranch).<br>6. F is the firing relation of an executor with output validation enabled. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] [ToString N] Build F ↦<br>  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N),<br>      OutputSpec t = some o →<br>        DupBranch o →<br>          Build t ≠ none ∧<br>            (InputsDistinct t →<br>              ∀ (p : N), firstDup o = some p → Build t = some (msgDup t p))) ∧<br>    ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (l r : Out N),<br>      OutputSpec t = some (l.and r) →<br>        Builds t →<br>          (l.and r).findTimeout = none →<br>            (∀ c ∈ andChildren (l.and r),<br>                ∀ (d : List N),<br>                  on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x x_1 ↦ ChildReceived d c) ∧<br>              ∀ (d : List N),<br>                on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x x_1 ↦<br>                  IO_011_uniqueBranch (l.and r) d</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The model is the pair (Build, F). Build is the transition builder, read as the message building a transition fails with, none when it builds; the witness is the reference builder refBuild, which runs the build-time checks of the Output Composition preamble in order (glossary: Build-time checks). The build error is msgDup, byte for byte: the message of the paragraph 'A place at most once per branch' with <t> the transition's name and <P> the place the walk reports. The preamble does not place the CORE-030 input-arc check among the output-spec checks; refBuild runs it first, so on a transition with two input arcs on one place the build line only requires that building fails.
- The walk is firstDup, through dupScan, clause for clause: steps 1 and 2 are its place, forward and timeout clauses, step 3 its xor clause (xorJoin: the first child's report, else the second's, else the places of both, each once, in first-seen order), step 4 its and clause (andStep at each child: the child's report, else the first place the child names that an earlier child of the And names, else the child's places join the pool). The reference Out is binary: an n-ary And or Xor is right-nested, and dupScan walks a .and met as a child of a .and as part of its parent, so the children of And(c1, ..., cn) are walked as the n-ary node's children. A spec with an And written as a direct child of an And is the one shape the binary Out cannot tell from the flat n-ary node; on it the builders walk the inner And first, the loader's walk (Load.dupWalk, over the spec as written) does the same, and firstDup reads the flat node.
- 'The walk reports a place exactly when some output branch names a place twice' is the theorem firstDup_eq_none_iff, and the place it reports is named twice in some output branch (firstDup_some); the build line therefore names a place exactly when it rejects a duplicate. AC5's And(Xor(A, B), Xor(B, A)) names B (IO_011_ac5); naming the first place repeated in the first repeating output branch, in IO-016 order, would name A, and that builder violates the build line (IO_011_forbids_firstBranchName).
- 'Which place the message names' is a constraint on the message only: which transitions build is the first build line together with the other build-time checks, and IO-013's third line ties Builds to the builder.
- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking that deposits the tokens of one outcome (glossary: Fire, Deposit); timing and priority play no part. A completion that fails validation (AC3) deposits no outcome and is not a firing here.
- Scope: 'applies to an executor with output validation enabled' is read as a restriction on the model: F is the firing relation of such an executor. An executor with the CONC-026 skip enabled is not an F this block speaks of; what it does (an And(A, B) firing that deposits only A, CONC-026 AC4) is exactly what io011_leftOnlyFire models, and this block forbids it of a validating executor (IO_011_forbids_leftOnly).
- Scope: the statement quantifies over transitions that build. Building runs the checks of the Output Composition preamble (WellFormedOut: no place twice in one output branch, at most one Timeout node, no Xor node inside the Timeout child, no ForwardInput outside a Timeout child, every ForwardInput's from an input place, no two output branches at different indices with the same set of places). CORE-022 applies to transitions that build, and a definition the checks reject is not a transition of any net. Build reads one transition definition, so the construction paths that reach the builder are not encoded.
- The reference semantics reads an n-ary And as right-nested binary `Out.and` nodes; `andChildren` flattens them back, so the children of And(A, B, C) are A, B and C. Flattening is the recursive reading of 'ALL child output specs must receive tokens': a nested And has received tokens exactly when each of its own children has.
- 'Received tokens' and 'the produced set' are read on the set of places the deposit touches (Produced, membership in d), and an output branch is read as a set of places (glossary: Output branch): several tokens into one place of the chosen branch conform, and the order of the deposit plays no part (CORE-036). How many tokens is IO-016's subject. Places outside the output spec are not constrained here (CORE-035 ties the marking to the deposit).
- 'Fires and its action completes' is read on output specs with no Timeout node (findTimeout = none), where every firing ends with its action. An output spec with its one Timeout node anywhere in it, such as And(A, Timeout(d, B)), is IO-013's: the deposit alone cannot tell the timeout outcome from the action outcome, so this block does not restate it there.
- The second clause is the recursive reading of 'ALL children receive tokens' below an Xor: an And under the Xor alternative the action chose must give every one of its children tokens, and the Xor itself must take one alternative (IO-012). Without it a deposit [0, 3] for And(Xor(And(0, 1), 2), 3) (a partial write of the chosen alternative) and a deposit [0, 1, 2] for And(0, Xor(1, 2)) (both alternatives) satisfy the first clause.
- 'That output branch is unique' is read by index: one entry of the IO-016 branch list, one per assignment. On a transition that builds it follows from the existence half and the last build check (IO_011_unique_of_wf), so it adds no constraint on F beyond the scope; no weaker mutant can tell it apart, and the evidence for it is that lemma and IO_011_reference_ambiguous, which shows that without the scope the reference fires And(Q, Xor(P, P)) with two exact branches.
- AC1 (requires at least 1 child) is structural and not encoded: the reference output spec has binary And nodes only, so an And with no child cannot be written; an And with one child is that child.
- AC2 (after the action completes, all children have received tokens) is the first clause, and the second at every depth.
- AC3 (validation failure if a child received no tokens) is read on firings: a completion in which a child received no tokens is not a firing, so the marking never shows it. That the failure is reported (an ActionFailed outcome, the error the user sees) is not observable in the firing relation, which has no events or errors; it is EXEC-030's and IO-015's subject and is not encoded here.
- AC4 and the paragraph 'A place at most once per branch' are the build line, and the first build check of WellFormedOut (every output branch Nodup, equivalently no BranchDuplicate, IO_011_noDuplicate_of_wf) for the firing lines' scope: And(P, P), And(P, And(Q, P)) and Xor(A, And(P, P)) are rejected with the message naming P, and Xor(And(P, A), And(P, B)) builds (IO_011_ac4). The construction paths and MOD-020's more specific message are not in the model. The reference semantics still fires And(P, P) depositing two tokens in P (IO_011_reference_fires_duplicate); that transition does not build, so the firing lines do not speak of it.
- AC5 (And(Xor(A, B), Xor(B, A)) is rejected naming B) is the build line on that spec, checked under the reference builder (IO_011_ac5).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): The reference semantics satisfies IO-011: the reference builder names the walk's place (`IO_011_refBuild_names`), and with no Timeout node the only outcome a reference firing can take is an action branch (`deposit` of a `timedOut` choice needs `findTimeout`), and every action branch of an `And` is the concatenation of one branch per child (`Out.branches`, `cross`), each of which contains a place its child names (`branch_receives_andChildren`); the deposit is itself the branch at its index, so it is exact for itself, and on a transition that builds no other index is (`IO_011_unique_of_wf`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N], IO_011 refBuild RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_forbids_bothArms` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): ✗ Writing both alternatives of an inner `Xor` violates IO-011: `[0, 1, 2]` matches neither output branch `[0, 1]` nor `[0, 2]` of `And(0, Xor(1, 2))`.
  <pre>¬IO_011 refBuild io011_bothArmsFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_forbids_firstBranchName` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): ✗ Naming the first place repeated in the first repeating output branch violates IO-011: the branches of `And(Xor(A, B), Xor(B, A))` are `[A, B]`, `[A, A]`, `[B, B]`, `[B, A]`, so it names `A`, not `B`.
  <pre>¬IO_011 io011_firstBranchBuild RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_forbids_leftOnly` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): ✗ Writing one child of an `And` only violates IO-011: `io011_t` deposits `[0]`, and its child `Place(1)` receives no token.
  <pre>¬IO_011 refBuild io011_leftOnlyFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_forbids_partialXorAnd` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): ✗ A partial write of the `And` an inner `Xor` chose violates IO-011: `[0, 3]` matches no output branch of `And(Xor(And(0, 1), 2), 3)` on its places (the branches are `[0, 1, 3]` and `[2, 3]`).
  <pre>¬IO_011 refBuild io011_partialFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_forbids_shallow` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): ✗ Writing only part of a nested `And` violates IO-011: `io011_nested` deposits `[0, 1]`, and its flattened child `Place(2)` receives no token.
  <pre>¬IO_011 refBuild io011_shallowFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_mutant_anyDupName_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): The first-branch builder kills the any-place mutant: every name it reports is a place named twice in one output branch, and its firings are the reference's, so the mutant holds; on AC5's spec it names `A`, not the walk's `B`, so IO-011 does not.
  <pre>IO_011_mutant_anyDupName io011_firstBranchBuild RefFire ∧<br>  ¬IO_011 io011_firstBranchBuild RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_mutant_childrenOnly_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): The partial firing kills the children-only mutant: both flattened children of `io011_xorAnd`, the `Xor` and `Place(3)`, received a token from `[0, 3]`, so the mutant holds; IO-011's second clause does not.
  <pre>IO_011_mutant_childrenOnly refBuild io011_partialFire ∧ ¬IO_011 refBuild io011_partialFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_mutant_noFlatten_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): The shallow firing kills the no-flatten mutant: its one firing writes `Place(0)` and place `1` of `And(Place(1), Place(2))`, so both immediate children received tokens and the mutant holds; the flattened child `Place(2)` received none, so IO-011 does not.
  <pre>IO_011_mutant_noFlatten refBuild io011_shallowFire ∧ ¬IO_011 refBuild io011_shallowFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_mutant_someChild_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): The left-only firing kills the "some child" mutant: its one firing writes `Place(0)`, a child of `io011_t`, so the mutant holds; it does not write `Place(1)`, so IO-011 does not.
  <pre>IO_011_mutant_someChild refBuild io011_leftOnlyFire ∧ ¬IO_011 refBuild io011_leftOnlyFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): IO-011 is exercised by the reference semantics: `io011_t` builds, has the output spec `And(Place(0), Place(1))` with no Timeout node, fires from the empty marking depositing `[0, 1]`, and each of its two children received a token (the conclusion is checked on a real firing, not vacuously).
  <pre>OutputSpec io011_t = some ((Out.place 0).and (Out.place 1)) ∧<br>  Builds io011_t ∧<br>    andChildren ((Out.place 0).and (Out.place 1)) = [Out.place 0, Out.place 1] ∧<br>      RefFire io011_t io011_empty [0, 1] io011_after ∧<br>        ChildReceived [0, 1] (Out.place 0) ∧ ChildReceived [0, 1] (Out.place 1)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_nonvacuous_name` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): The build line is exercised by the reference: AC5's spec names a place twice in one output branch, the walk reports `B` (`1`), and the reference builder rejects the transition with the message naming it.
  <pre>OutputSpec io011_cross =<br>    some (((Out.place 0).xor (Out.place 1)).and ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.place 0))) ∧<br>  DupBranch (((Out.place 0).xor (Out.place 1)).and ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.place 0))) ∧<br>    InputsDistinct io011_cross ∧<br>      firstDup (((Out.place 0).xor (Out.place 1)).and ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.place 0))) =<br>          some 1 ∧<br>        refBuild io011_cross = some (msgDup io011_cross 1)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_011_nonvacuous_xor` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_011): The second clause is exercised by the reference semantics: `io011_andXor` builds and fires from the empty marking depositing `[0, 1]`, which is exact for its output branch at index 0, `[0, 1]`, and for no other index.
  <pre>OutputSpec io011_andXor = some ((Out.place 0).and ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.place 2))) ∧<br>  Builds io011_andXor ∧<br>    RefFire io011_andXor io011_empty [0, 1] io011_after ∧<br>      IO_011_uniqueBranch ((Out.place 0).and ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.place 2))) [0, 1]</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_scoped` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✓ The validating blocks and the skip hold together, each of its own executor: IO-010, IO-011 and IO-012 hold of the reference firings (output validation enabled), CONC-026 of the skip-validation firings, and every reference firing of a transition that builds is a skip-validation firing, so enabling the option only adds firings.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  IO_010 RefFire ∧<br>    IO_011 refBuild RefFire ∧<br>      IO_012 IO_012_check refBuild RefFire ∧<br>        CONC_026 refFireSkipValidation ∧<br>          ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (m : N → ℕ) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            Builds t → RefFire t m d m' → refFireSkipValidation t m d m'</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_012"></a>
## IO-012: Output Xor

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_012` · Kind **B** · Scope build check: every transition, on every construction path; firing lines: executors with output validation enabled (the default, CONC-026) · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `6faa14aabd5ce354`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| `Xor(children)` is an output spec node with two or more children; it represents a decision point where the action chooses one child.<br><br>*When* a transition whose output spec is `Xor(children)` fires and the firing ends with its action, its produced set *shall* equal, on the places the output spec names, one output branch of the output spec.<br><br>*When* a transition whose output spec is `Xor(children)` fires and the firing ends with its action, its produced set *shall not* equal, on the places the output spec names, two output branches that take different children at some `Xor` node.<br><br>This requirement applies to an executor with output validation enabled, which is the default ([CONC-026]).<br><br>A firing ends with its action when it does not end with the timeout outcome ([IO-013]).<br><br>Deposits are compared per place ([CORE-036]): a firing whose deposit puts in every place as many tokens as some timeout-outcome deposit of the transition from the same marking may have ended with the timeout outcome, and the two lines above do not constrain it.<br><br>An *assignment* of an output spec chooses one child at each `Xor` node it reaches ([IO-015]).<br><br>`Xor` nodes are told apart by their position in the spec, never by what they contain, so `Xor(P, P)` has two assignments.<br><br>Two assignments are *distinct* when they choose different children at some `Xor` node that both reach.<br><br>*If* two distinct assignments of a transition's output spec claim the same set of places, at any depth of the spec, *then* building the transition *shall* fail with<br><br>Enumerate the output branches of the spec per [IO-016], numbered from 0 in enumeration order (the numbering of the virtual transitions `&lt;t>_b&lt;i>` of [VER-001]).<br><br>The enumeration lists exactly one branch per assignment, so two branches at different indices come from distinct assignments.<br><br>Read each branch as the set of places it names, a `ForwardInput(from, to)` naming `to`.<br><br>Visit the branches in index order.<br><br>The first branch `j` whose set equals the set of an earlier branch fails the check, and `i` is the smallest index of such an earlier branch.<br><br>If no branch fails, the check passes.<br><br>`&lt;places>` lists the places of branch `i` by name, separated by `, `, in the order the spec as written names them (leaves left to right).<br><br>An implementation MAY decide the check without enumerating every branch, provided it reports the same `i`, `j` and `&lt;places>`.<br><br>A transition that builds therefore has pairwise distinct output branches, so at most one output branch equals any produced set, and the second line above holds of every firing of such a transition. | 1. Build check. For every output spec o (the output spec of the transition being built), with bs its output branches (Out.branches, one entry per assignment, numbered from 0):<br>2. the check C passes (C o = none) exactly when no two entries of bs at indices i &lt; j claim the same set of places (IO_012_unambiguous; IO_012_clash compares the entries as sets, SameSet);<br>3. the check fails reporting (i, j, ps) (C o = some (i, j, ps)) exactly when the entries i &lt; j clash, no entry before j clashes with an earlier one, no entry before i clashes with entry j, and ps is entry i as a list (IO_012_reported).<br>4. Building. For every transition t whose output spec o is ambiguous (not IO_012_unambiguous): building t fails (Build t ≠ none); and when every earlier build-time check passes (input places distinct, CORE-030; no output branch naming a place twice, IO-011; at most one Timeout node and no Xor node inside its child, IO-013; no ForwardInput outside a Timeout child and every forward from an input place, IO-014), building fails with IO-012's message for the pair (i, j) and the places ps the check reports (msgAmbiguous t i j ps).<br>5. Firing. For every transition t whose output spec is Xor(l, r) and passes the check, every deposit d, and every firing of t under F from m to m' that deposits d and whose deposit is not, place by place, a timeout-outcome deposit of t from m (IO_012_timeoutLike): some output branch b of Xor(l, r) is exact for d: on every place q the output spec names, d puts one or more tokens in q exactly when q is in b (ExactOn).<br>6. The second firing line is a consequence, not a clause: on an output spec that passes the check two exact branches would be the same set (both lie within outPlaces), so at most one is exact (theorem IO_012_unique).<br>7. `on R, shall Q` reads: every step from m to m' with R m m' satisfies Q m m'; here Q ignores both markings. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] [ToString N] C Build F ↦<br>  (∀ (o : Out N), C o = none ↔ IO_012_unambiguous o) ∧<br>    (∀ (o : Out N) (i j : ℕ) (ps : List N),<br>        C o = some (i, j, ps) ↔ IO_012_reported o i j ps) ∧<br>      (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N),<br>          OutputSpec t = some o →<br>            ¬IO_012_unambiguous o →<br>              Build t ≠ none ∧<br>                (InputsDistinct t →<br>                  ¬DupBranch o →<br>                    timeoutCount o ≤ 1 →<br>                      xorInTimeout o = false →<br>                        forwardOutside o = none →<br>                          (∀ s ∈ forwardSources o, s ∈ inPlaces t) →<br>                            ∀ (i j : ℕ) (ps : List N),<br>                              C o = some (i, j, ps) →<br>                                Build t = some (msgAmbiguous t i j ps))) ∧<br>        ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (l r : Out N),<br>          OutputSpec t = some (l.xor r) →<br>            IO_012_unambiguous (l.xor r) →<br>              ∀ (d : List N),<br>                on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m' ∧ ¬IO_012_timeoutLike t m d, shall fun x x_1 ↦<br>                  ∃ b ∈ (l.xor r).branches, ExactOn (l.xor r) d b</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking that deposits the tokens of one outcome (glossary: Fire, Deposit); timing and priority play no part. A completion that fails, by an action exception (EXEC-030) or by IO-015 validation, deposits no outcome and is not a firing here: an action failure does not end the firing with its action.
- F models an executor with output validation enabled, the default; an executor with the CONC-026 skip enabled is not constrained by the firing line (scope). The build check is independent of any executor: C is the check itself.
- The check is modelled by what it reports, not by its text: C o = some (i, j, ps) stands for the message `output spec of transition '<t>' is ambiguous: output branches <i> and <j> both claim {<places>}; no write can tell them apart (IO-012)`, with <places> the names of ps joined by ', '. The transition name, the rendering of names, the message text, and the claim that it is byte-identical across implementations are not in the model; the cross-language tests of AC5 pin them.
- The check C is stated for one output spec in isolation; the third line ties it to the builder. In the builder it runs last of the build-time checks (the Output Composition preamble: CORE-030's input check, then IO-011, IO-013's two checks, IO-014 before it), so a spec that an earlier check rejects reports that check's message instead: the third line asks for IO-012's message only when every earlier check passes, and otherwise only that building fails. The message is the glossary's msgAmbiguous, the text the markdown gives. That the check runs on every construction path (builders, composition, channel merge, fusion) and that no executor option skips it is read as: every transition in a net has an output spec C passes. The firing line is stated for such transitions; the model has no construction paths to enumerate.
- 'An implementation MAY decide the check without enumerating every branch' is the extensional reading: the block constrains C only by what it returns, so any algorithm that returns the same (i, j, ps) satisfies it. IO_012_check is one such algorithm, the enumeration of the numbered steps.
- Place identity is equality on N, the implementation's own Place equality (names in TypeScript, Rust and Python, name and token type in Java; CORE-002, MOD-024). A ForwardInput(from, to) claims `to`, a Timeout its child's claim (Out.branches).
- Branches are numbered by the reference enumeration Out.branches over the right-nested binary Out: an n-ary Xor(c₁, …, cₙ) is xor c₁ (xor c₂ … cₙ) and concatenates its children's lists in child order; an n-ary And is the cartesian product with the first child varying slowest. These are IO-016's lists and VER-001's <t>_b<i> numbering, so the binary nesting changes no index.
- 'Receives tokens' is read as IO-015's exact explanation, as AC4 directs: a child whose output branch is a strict subset of the produced set has received tokens but is not satisfied. Read literally, `Xor(And(A, B, C), And(A, B))` writing {A, B} would have two children receiving tokens; under the exact reading only the second child's branch equals it.
- The produced set is compared on the places the output spec names (outPlaces), as IO-015 compares on declared places: a token in a place the spec never names is CORE-072's business. A token in a named place outside the selected branch still breaks the firing line (IO-015 AC9). A branch fixes which places receive tokens, not how many (glossary: Output branch): several tokens into one place of the branch conform.
- A firing ends with its action unless it is the timeout outcome (glossary: Timeout outcome, IO-013); the abstract firing relation does not label the outcome, so a firing whose deposit puts in every place as many tokens as some timeout-outcome deposit of t from the same marking is not checked (IO-012), whatever the order of its tokens. When the action happens to write such a deposit, that firing is not checked either. An Xor that contains a Timeout node is otherwise covered.
- The firing line covers a transition whose whole output spec is an Xor. An Xor nested inside And or Timeout is covered by IO-015, which validates every Xor an assignment reaches. The build check covers every output spec, an Xor at any depth included: And(Q, Xor(P, P)) is rejected.
- AC1 (at least two children) is structural: the binary xor node has the two children l and r, and an n-ary Xor nests as xor c₁ (xor c₂ … cₙ) (checked by example below); the reference loader rejects an xor with fewer than two children.
- AC2 (exactly one child received tokens) is the firing line (at least one exact branch, so one child of the top Xor) together with its consequence IO_012_unique (not two). An output branch takes exactly one child of the top Xor.
- AC3 (zero branches satisfied → validation error) is the firing line: such a completion is not a firing. The test derivation's 'action produces to both' case is also the firing line under the exact reading: Xor(P1, P2) has no output branch {P1, P2}.
- AC4 (two branches whose claims are the same set never reach a firing) is the build check: such a spec does not pass C, and the firing line is stated only for specs that pass. The strict-subset remark of AC4 is the exact reading above: Xor(And(A, B, C), And(A, B)) passes the check (checked by example below).
- AC5 (the rejected and accepted specs and their reported pairs and places) is checked by example on IO_012_check below, with the places A, B, C, P, Q, a named by the numbers 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5. The Timeout duration d is not in the reference Out. The composition case (two output ports in different alternatives bound to one host place) is the check applied to the composed spec; composition itself is MOD-024's model, not this block's.
- The Test derivation's build cases are AC5's; its firing cases are AC3's.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✓ IO-012 holds of the reference: the reference check (`IO_012_check`) reports exactly what the block names, the reference builder (`refBuild`) rejects every ambiguous output spec with IO-012's message once the earlier checks pass, and the reference firings satisfy the firing line.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  IO_012 IO_012_check refBuild RefFire</pre>
- ✓ **allows** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_allows_permutedTimeout` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✓ IO-012 allows a firing whose deposit is a timeout-outcome deposit in another order ([CORE-036]): `[2, 1]` equals the timeout deposit `[1, 2]` place by place, so the firing may have ended with the timeout outcome and the firing line does not constrain it, although `[2, 1]` matches no output branch (`{0}`, `{1, 2, 3}`).
  <pre>IO_012 IO_012_check refBuild io012_permFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_forbids_acceptAll` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✗ A build check that accepts every output spec violates IO-012 (the first build clause, AC4/AC5): it passes `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))`, whose two output branches claim `{0}`.
  <pre>¬IO_012 (fun x ↦ none) refBuild RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_forbids_both` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✗ A firing that writes both children violates IO-012 (the firing line; the test derivation's "produces to both"): `Xor(Place(0), Place(1))` has no output branch `{0, 1}`.
  <pre>¬IO_012 IO_012_check refBuild (io012_oneFire ((Out.place 0).xor (Out.place 1)) [0, 1])</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_forbids_buildAll` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✗ A builder that builds every transition violates IO-012 (the build line): `io012_dup`, whose output spec `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))` is ambiguous, builds.
  <pre>¬IO_012 IO_012_check (fun x ↦ none) RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_forbids_buildVague` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✗ A builder that rejects an ambiguous output spec with another message violates IO-012 (the build line): `Xor(P, P)` passes every earlier check, so building it must fail with IO-012's message for branches 0 and 1 and `{P}`, not with `rejected`.
  <pre>¬IO_012 IO_012_check io012_buildVague RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_forbids_iMajor` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✗ A build check that reports the wrong pair violates IO-012 (the second build clause, step 3): on `io012_pqqp` the first clashing `j` is 2 (with `i` = 1), not 3.
  <pre>¬IO_012 io012_iMajor refBuild RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_forbids_silent` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✗ A firing that writes no child violates IO-012 (the firing line, AC3): `io012_t` fires with an empty deposit, and no output branch of `Xor(Place(0), Place(1))` is empty.
  <pre>¬IO_012 IO_012_check refBuild (io012_oneFire ((Out.place 0).xor (Out.place 1)) [])</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_forbids_silentWithTimeout` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): ✗ An `Xor` with a `Timeout` child is checked too (the firing line): `Xor(Place(0), Timeout(Place(1)))` firing with an empty deposit is not, place by place, a timeout outcome (that puts a token in `1`) and matches no output branch.
  <pre>¬IO_012 IO_012_check refBuild (io012_oneFire ((Out.place 0).xor (Out.place 1).timeout) [])</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_mutant_anyMessage_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): The vague builder kills the any-message mutant: it rejects whatever the reference builder rejects, but not with IO-012's message.
  <pre>IO_012_mutant_anyMessage IO_012_check io012_buildVague RefFire ∧<br>  ¬IO_012 IO_012_check io012_buildVague RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_mutant_anyPair_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): The `i`-first check kills the third mutant: it reports a clashing pair with its places, so the mutant holds, but on `io012_pqqp` it is not the pair step 3 names.
  <pre>IO_012_mutant_anyPair io012_iMajor refBuild RefFire ∧<br>  ¬IO_012 io012_iMajor refBuild RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_mutant_dropBuildLine_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): The build-everything builder kills the build-line mutant: the reference check and firings satisfy every other line, but `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))` builds.
  <pre>IO_012_mutant_dropBuildLine IO_012_check (fun x ↦ none) RefFire ∧<br>  ¬IO_012 IO_012_check (fun x ↦ none) RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_mutant_dropBuild_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): The accept-everything check kills the first mutant: the reference firings satisfy the firing line, so the mutant holds, but `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))` builds and IO-012 fails.

- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_012_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_012): The build check and the firing line of IO-012 are exercised by the reference: `io012_t` has the output spec `Xor(Place(0), Place(1))`, which the reference check passes; it fires from the empty marking writing place `0`, which is not a timeout outcome (the trigger of the firing line); the branch `{0}` is exact, the branch `{1}` is not; and the reference check rejects `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))`, the output spec of `io012_dup`, reporting branches 0 and 1 and the places `[0]`.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_013"></a>
## IO-013: Output timeout

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_013` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `3b6ff92649b90b7a`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| `Timeout(duration, child)` is a node of an output spec that gives the action of a firing a budget of `duration` to complete.<br><br>*If* the action does not complete within `duration`, the firing *shall* end with the timeout outcome: it is abandoned, and it deposits the tokens of `child`.<br><br>*When* a firing ends with the timeout outcome, it *shall not* deposit any output of the action, so the marking receives the tokens of `child` and nothing else.<br><br>*If* an output spec holds more than one `Timeout` node, whether one is nested in the child of another or they sit apart, *then* building the transition *shall* fail with<br><br>where `&lt;n>` is the number of `Timeout` nodes in the spec.<br><br>The check runs when the transition is built, on every construction path, as the check of [IO-011] does.<br><br>A transition that builds therefore has at most one timeout outcome, and the `child` of its `Timeout` holds no `Timeout` node.<br><br>The executor, not the action, makes the deposit of the timeout outcome, so nothing chooses among alternatives inside the `Timeout` child.<br><br>*If* the child of an output spec's `Timeout` node holds an `Xor` node at any depth, *then* building the transition *shall* fail with<br><br>The check runs after the `Timeout` count check above, so the spec it sees holds at most one `Timeout` node.<br><br>The timeout outcome of a transition that builds therefore deposits in one fixed set of places: every place the `Timeout` child names. | 1. Let Build be the builder (Build t = none: t builds; Build t = some msg: building t fails with msg), F the firings and T the firings that end with the timeout outcome.<br>2. For every transition t whose output spec o holds two or more Timeout nodes, building t fails; and if t's input places are distinct and no output branch of o names a place twice, it fails with the IO-013 message for t and the number of Timeout nodes in o.<br>3. Every transition that passes every build check (distinct input places, no place twice in one output branch, at most one Timeout node, no Xor node inside the Timeout child, no ForwardInput outside a Timeout child, every forward from an input place, no two output branches claiming one set of places) builds.<br>4. For every transition t that builds: every firing of t in T is a firing in F, with the same deposit and target marking.<br>5. For every transition t that builds: every firing of t in T from m deposits, place by place, exactly the tokens of the timeout outcome of t (for what t consumes at m), and nothing else.<br>6. For every transition t that builds: from every marking m at which t is enabled, every timeout outcome d of t (for what t consumes at m) is the deposit of some firing in T.<br>7. For every transition t that builds and whose output spec has a Timeout node, every firing of t in F is in T, or has as its produced set exactly the places of one output branch of t.<br>8. For every transition t whose output spec o holds at most one Timeout node and an Xor node at any depth of that node's child (xorInTimeout), building t fails; and if t's input places are distinct and no output branch of o names a place twice, it fails with IO-013's Xor message for t (msgXorInTimeout). | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] Build F T ↦<br>  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N),<br> shall fun m m' ↦ F t m d m') ∧<br>        (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N),<br>            Build t = none →<br>              ∀ (d : List N),<br>                on fun m m' ↦ T t m d m', shall fun m x ↦<br> Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N),<br>                OutputSpec t = some o →<br>                  timeoutCount o ≤ 1 →<br>                    xorInTimeout o = true →<br>                      Build t ≠ none ∧<br>                        (InputsDistinct t →<br>                          ¬DupBranch o → Build t = some (msgXorInTimeout t))</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The model is the triple (Build, F, T). Build is the transition builder, read as the message building a transition fails with, none when it builds; the reference builder refBuild runs the checks the model can see in the order of the output composition preamble of spec/02-input-output-specs.md. F is the firing relation and T the firings that end with the timeout outcome (glossary: Timeout outcome), each read as one step from a marking to a marking that deposits the tokens of one outcome (glossary: Fire, Deposit); timing and priority play no part. A plain FireRel cannot carry T: on Xor(Place 0, Timeout(Place 1)) the timeout deposit [1] is also the action branch {1}, so a timed-out firing that deposits the sibling branch [0] would look like an action firing. The witness triple is refBuild, RefFire and refTimeoutOutcomeFire.
- The build error is msgTimeouts, byte for byte: output spec of transition '<t>' has <n> Timeout nodes; an output spec has at most one, nested or not (IO-013), with <n> the number of Timeout nodes in the spec, nested and sibling ones alike (timeoutCount). The preamble's order puts the IO-011 check (a place twice in one output branch) first, so on such a spec the message is IO-011's and the second line only requires that building fails. The preamble does not place the CORE-030 input-arc check, so on a transition with two input arcs on one place the second line likewise only requires that building fails.
- 'On every construction path' is read as: Build is the one builder every construction path goes through (builders, composition, channel merge, fusion), so a transition that a path yields builds exactly when Build accepts it. Which paths exist is not in the model.
- The third line is the converse that makes 'builds' checkable (AC7's specs that build): a transition passing every build check of the output composition preamble and CORE-030 builds. The checks are the glossary terms Builds and WellFormedOut, the build-time checks of IO-011, IO-012, IO-013 and IO-014; checks the model cannot see (AC1's positive duration, a spec that names no place) are outside it.
- A transition that builds has at most one Timeout node, so its timeout outcome is the one Timeout node's child (Out.findTimeout finds it), and that child holds no Timeout node. The firing lines are stated for transitions that build only: a transition building rejects is not a transition of any net (CORE-022) and never fires.
- The duration is not in the model, so whether the budget expires is nondeterministic: from every marking at which t is enabled, the action may overrun it. The sixth line therefore reads 'if the action does not complete within duration, the firing shall end with the timeout outcome' as 'the timeout outcome is always one of the ways a firing of t can end'. That overrunning forces the timeout, and that completing in time rules it out, are statements about time that a model of untimed steps cannot express.
- 'It is abandoned' is the action's output being excluded, which the fifth line states for the deposit. Whether the action is stopped is the side-effect paragraph that follows the first paragraph, not encoded.
- 'The marking receives the tokens of child and nothing else' is read at the deposit (fifth line). Which marking a firing leads to is the effect rule of the glossary term Fire (CORE-030, CORE-034, CORE-035); the fourth line ties every timed-out firing to a firing of F, so that rule applies to it.
- 'The tokens of child' is the glossary term Timeout outcome, as the reference reads it (Out.timeoutDeposits): one token per Place leaf of child, the product at every And, and one token in to per token consumed from from at every ForwardInput (IO-014), counted per place, so the order of the deposit list does not matter (CORE-036). Token values and created_at (the minted tokens of AC3, forwarded values and their stamps, IO-014 AC5) are below the count granularity.
- The eighth line is the paragraph 'One fixed timeout deposit'. Its message is msgXorInTimeout, byte for byte. Any Xor at any depth of the Timeout child counts, under an And or a ForwardInput's sibling included (hasXor). It runs after the Timeout count check, as the premise timeoutCount o ≤ 1 states: with two Timeout nodes the message is the first line's. The preamble's order puts the IO-011 check before it, so on a spec that names a place twice in one output branch the eighth line only requires that building fails, and on a transition with two input arcs on one place likewise (CORE-030). 'The executor, not the action, makes the deposit' is the reason the markdown gives and is not a separate constraint.
- 'The timeout outcome of a transition that builds therefore deposits in one fixed set of places' is a consequence, not a clause: on a transition that builds the Timeout child holds no Xor node, so Out.timeoutDeposits of the child has one entry and the fifth and sixth lines name one deposit (theorem IO_013_one_fixed_deposit, from the glossary's timeoutOutcome_unique). Without the check the reference offers one timeout outcome per alternative of an Xor in the child (IO_013_reference_xorChild_two_outcomes).
- The seventh line is AC5 for a firing that does not report the timeout outcome: it ended with its action, whose produced set is one output branch (IO-015, exact explanation; how many tokens per place is IO-016's). A deposit that merges a timeout outcome with output the action wrote and reports it as an action firing is neither, unless its places happen to form a whole output branch, in which case it is a legal action firing and no observation of the marking can tell the two apart. The line is a requirement on an executor with output validation enabled, the default (CONC-026); F models such an executor, as RefFire does. With the skip enabled an action firing deposits the action's write and the line is not claimed of it, while the timed-out lines hold under every executor: CONC-026 skips nothing on the timeout outcome.
- AC1 (duration must be positive) is a build-time validation of the duration, which the model does not carry; not encoded.
- AC2 (action completes within duration, normal output validation) is the produced-set disjunct of the seventh line; the validation itself is IO-015's subject.
- AC3 (action exceeds duration, every place the timeout child names receives tokens: one minted token for a Place, the forwarded tokens for a ForwardInput) is the fifth and sixth lines, read on token counts.
- AC4 (an ActionTimedOut event is emitted on timeout) is an event-store obligation (EVT-009); the firing relation has no events, so it is not encoded.
- AC5 (output produced before the budget expired is discarded, the marking receiving the tokens of the timeout child's one fixed set of places and nothing else; a partial write under an Xor sibling is not a second satisfied branch) is the fifth line for a firing in T and the seventh for a firing outside it; 'one fixed set' is IO_013_one_fixed_deposit.
- AC6 (a TimeoutException the action raises is an ordinary action failure, not the timeout branch) is about which trigger ends the firing; an action failure deposits no outcome (EXEC-030) and is not a firing in this model, so it is not encoded.
- AC7 is the second and third lines: its three rejected specs (Timeout(d, Timeout(e, P)), Xor(Timeout(d, A), Timeout(e, B)), And(Timeout(d, A), Xor(B, Timeout(e, C)))) fail with the message for n = 2 and its two accepted specs (Xor(A, Timeout(d, B)), Timeout(d, And(A, B))) build, under the reference builder (IO_013_ac7_rejected, IO_013_ac7_builds).
- AC8 is the eighth and third lines: its two rejected specs (Timeout(d, Xor(A, B)), Xor(C, Timeout(d, And(A, Xor(B, D))))) fail with the Xor message, and its two accepted specs (Xor(A, Timeout(d, B)) and, with i an input place, Timeout(d, And(A, ForwardInput(i, B)))) build, under the reference builder (IO_013_ac8_rejected, IO_013_ac8_builds).
- The test derivation's build cases are AC7's and AC8's; its timing case (an action sleeping past its budget, the event) is outside the model: the duration and the event store are not in it.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_013_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_013): The reference satisfies IO-013: the reference builder rejects a spec with two or more `Timeout` nodes with IO-013's message and accepts every transition that passes every build check; a timed-out reference firing is a reference firing, deposits the timeout outcome and nothing else (`.timedOut j`), is available from every enabled marking, and every other reference firing ends with its action, depositing one output branch (`.action i`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  IO_013 refBuild RefFire refTimeoutOutcomeFire</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_014"></a>
## IO-014: Output ForwardInput

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_014` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `19e7743aee1c508e`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| `ForwardInput(from, to)` is a node of an output spec that, on the timeout outcome, deposits in `to` the tokens a firing consumed from its input place `from`.<br><br>*If* a `ForwardInput` node of an output spec is not inside the child of a `Timeout` node, *then* building the transition *shall* fail with<br><br>naming the first such node in the order the spec is written (pre-order, children left to right).<br><br>The check runs when the transition is built, on every construction path, as the check of [IO-011] does.<br><br>*When* a firing ends with the timeout outcome, every `ForwardInput(from, to)` in the `Timeout` child *shall* deposit in `to` one token for each token the firing consumed from `from`, carrying the token's value, in consumption order.<br><br>The count *shall* be the consumption count of the input arc on `from` ([IO-007]): 1 for `One`, `n` for `Exactly(n)`, and the whole drained batch for `All` and `AtLeast(m)`.<br><br>*If* a firing consumed more than one token from `from`, *then* the forward *shall not* deposit fewer tokens in `to` than the firing consumed; in particular it *shall not* forward only the first consumed token.<br><br>*If* a firing consumed no token from `from`, which a declared input cannot do but a mis-declared spec can, *then* the forward *shall* deposit no token in `to`. | 1. Let Build be the builder (Build t = none: t builds; Build t = some msg: building t fails with msg) and T the firings that end with the timeout outcome.<br>2. For every transition t whose output spec o has a ForwardInput(from, to) outside the child of a Timeout node, the first in pre-order, building t fails;<br>3. and if t's input places are distinct, no output branch of o names a place twice, o has at most one Timeout node and no Xor node inside its Timeout child, building t fails with the IO-014 message naming t, from and to.<br>4. For every transition t whose output spec has a ForwardInput from a place that is not one of t's input places, building t fails.<br>5. Every transition that passes every build check (distinct input places, no place twice in one output branch, at most one Timeout node, no Xor node inside the Timeout child, no ForwardInput outside a Timeout child, every forward from an input place, no two output branches claiming one set of places) builds.<br>6. For every transition t whose output spec has at most one Timeout node and a ForwardInput(from, to) anywhere in that node's child (also under And or Xor), every deposit d and every firing of t from m in T that deposits d, there is one timeout deposit e of the Timeout child, built with every ForwardInput(a, b) of the child putting in b one token per token the firing consumes from a, such that e puts no more tokens than d in any place (no consumed token is lost, at any forward of the child) and d puts no more tokens than e in any place (one token per consumed token, none extra). | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] [ToString N] Build T ↦<br>  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N) (src dst : N),<br>      OutputSpec t = some o → forwardOutside o = some (src, dst) → Build t ≠ none) ∧<br>    (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N) (src dst : N),<br>        OutputSpec t = some o →<br>          forwardOutside o = some (src, dst) →<br>            InputsDistinct t →<br>              ¬DupBranch o →<br>                timeoutCount o ≤ 1 →<br>                  xorInTimeout o = false → Build t = some (msgForward t src dst)) ∧<br>      (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N) (src : N),<br>          OutputSpec t = some o →<br>            src ∈ forwardSources o → src ∉ inPlaces t → Build t ≠ none) ∧<br>        (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N), Builds t → Build t = none) ∧<br>          ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (src dst : N),<br>            (∀ (o : Out N), OutputSpec t = some o → timeoutCount o ≤ 1) →<br>              TimeoutForwards t src dst →<br>                ∀ (d : List N),<br>                  on fun m m' ↦ T t m d m', shall fun m x ↦<br>                    ∃ e,<br>                      TimeoutOutcome t (Consumes t m) e ∧<br>                        (∀ (p : N), Deposits e p ≤ Deposits d p) ∧<br>                          ∀ (p : N), Deposits d p ≤ Deposits e p</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The model is the pair (Build, T). Build is the transition builder, read as the message building a transition fails with, none when it builds; the reference builder refBuild runs the CORE-030 input-arc check and then the output-spec checks in the order of the output composition preamble of spec/02-input-output-specs.md (IO-011, IO-013's Timeout count, IO-013's Xor inside the Timeout child, IO-014, IO-014 AC3, IO-012), with each message byte for byte except CORE-030's, which the spec does not give. Place names are rendered with the place type's ToString.
- T is the relation of firings that end with the timeout outcome (glossary: Timeout outcome), read as one step from a marking to a marking that deposits the Timeout child's tokens (glossary: Fire, Deposit); timing and priority play no part. A plain FireRel cannot be used: on Timeout(ForwardInput(from, to)) the action branch [to] and a forward of one token are the same deposit. The witness refTimedOutFire is the part of RefFire whose outcome is a timedOut choice (refTimedOutFire_refFire). That the resulting marking receives d is the effect rule (CORE-030, CORE-034, CORE-035) on the plain firing relation, not restated here.
- The build error is msgForward, byte for byte: output spec of transition '<t>' uses ForwardInput('<from>', '<to>') outside the child of a Timeout node; a forward deposits only on the timeout outcome (IO-014). 'Not inside the child of a Timeout node' and 'the first such node' are forwardOutside: the pre-order walk, children left to right, that does not enter a Timeout node. The preamble's order runs the IO-011 check (a place twice in one output branch) and the IO-013 checks (more than one Timeout node, an Xor node inside the Timeout child) first, so on such a spec the message is theirs, and the second line only requires that building fails; CORE-030's input-arc check is not placed by the preamble and is treated the same way.
- 'On every construction path' is read as: Build is the one builder every construction path goes through (builders, composition, channel merge, fusion), so a transition that a path yields builds exactly when Build accepts it. Which paths exist is not in the model. No executor option reaches Build (CONC-026).
- The fifth line is the converse that makes 'builds' checkable (AC7's specs that build): a transition passing every build check of the output composition preamble and CORE-030 builds. The checks are the glossary terms Builds and WellFormedOut, the same checks IO-013 reads.
- The firing line is stated for every transition whose output spec has at most one Timeout node, not only for transitions that build: a transition with two or more Timeout nodes is rejected (IO-013) and has no single Timeout child, so no reading of 'the Timeout child' is claimed there, while a transition with a forward from a non-input place, rejected by the fourth line, is still covered, which is what keeps the fourth quote (no input consumed, nothing forwarded) checkable. A transition with one Timeout node has one Timeout child, the one Out.findTimeout finds.
- 'The `ForwardInput(from, to)` node of an output spec that, on the timeout outcome, deposits in `to` the tokens a firing consumed' (first quote) is the firing line, which says how many.
- 'Every ForwardInput(from, to) in the Timeout child' includes a ForwardInput nested under And or Xor. On a transition that builds the child holds no Xor node (IO-013), so it has one timeout deposit (glossary: timeoutOutcome_unique); the line is also stated for transitions that do not build, where an Xor child has several timeout deposits and which one a firing chose is visible only in the whole deposit, so the statement compares d place by place with one timeout deposit e of the child (glossary: Timeout outcome, TimeoutOutcome); deposits are compared per place, so the order of the deposit list does not matter (CORE-036). This also fixes the tokens the child's Place leaves put in, which is IO-013's subject (AC5); it is restated here only because it is what identifies the chosen alternative. Comparing only the place `to` would let a forward-first firing pass whenever another alternative of the child happens to put the same count in `to`.
- 'The count' (sixth quote) is the firing's consumption from `from`, the glossary term Consume (Consumes t m from), which is IO-007's consumption count of the input arc on `from` (bridge lemma consumes_eq) and 0 for a place with no input arc. The two bounds of the firing line together say d equals e place by place, with one existential e for both; they are split so that losing tokens and inventing tokens are refuted separately.
- The seventh quote (no forward of fewer tokens, not only the first) is the first bound. The eighth quote (no input consumed, nothing forwarded) is the second bound, e having no forwarded token where Consumes t m from = 0.
- The sentence '*When* a `ForwardInput` forwards a consumed token, the token it deposits in `to` *shall* carry the consumed token's `created_at` as well as its value.' is token metadata (a value and a creation stamp) below the count granularity of the firing semantics, which has no token values or stamps; it is not encoded, and neither is the paragraph around it (the TIME-015 exception, the NU-022 tie-break).
- AC1 (from must be a declared input place, validated at build time) and AC3 (an invalid from reference is a build error) are the fourth line. Which forward AC3's existing message names when several are invalid is not said by the text; the reference builder names the first in pre-order, and the statement does not constrain the message.
- AC2 (one token per consumed token, preserving values and consumption order) is the firing line for its count; values and consumption order are below the count granularity and are not encoded.
- AC4 (All or Exactly(n) with N tokens consumed puts exactly N in `to`) is the two bounds together, with Consumes giving N for Exactly(n) and the whole place for All.
- AC5 (a forwarded token's created_at equals the original's and is not re-stamped from the epoch clock, while a minted recovery token is, in every implementation) is token metadata below the count granularity and is not encoded.
- AC6 (every analysis deposits what the executor deposits) is about the verifier's branch expansion (IO-016, VER-001), not about firings or building; Novel/ForwardDeposit.lean proves it for the flattened rows.
- AC7 is the second, third and fifth lines: its three rejected specs (ForwardInput(a, b), Xor(c, ForwardInput(a, b)), And(c, Xor(e, ForwardInput(a, b)))) fail with the message naming a and b, and its two accepted specs (Timeout(d, ForwardInput(a, b)), Xor(c, Timeout(d, And(e, ForwardInput(a, b))))) build, under the reference builder with a an input place (IO_014_ac7_rejected, IO_014_ac7_builds).

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_015"></a>
## IO-015: Output Validation

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_015` · Kind **F** · Scope every declared output spec · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `61ac6b8b57fcb0a7`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| An *assignment* selects exactly one child at each `Xor` node it reaches; nodes beneath an unselected `Xor` child are never evaluated.<br><br>*If* exactly one assignment claims a set *equal* to the produced set, *then* validation *shall* succeed.<br><br>*If* no assignment claims a set equal to the produced set, *then* validation *shall* fail, because nothing explains what was written.<br><br>*If* two or more assignments claim a set equal to the produced set, *then* validation *shall* fail, because the write is genuinely ambiguous.<br><br>For a transition that builds, no two assignments claim the same set ([IO-012]), so on a firing this third case does not arise.<br><br>The comparison is over **sets of places**: a spec names places, not counts, so an action that deposits several tokens into one claimed place conforms. | 1. For every declared output spec o and every deposit d, with assignment i of o claiming the i-th entry of Out.branches o:<br>2. if some assignment i claims exactly the produced set of d and every assignment that does is i, validation of d against o succeeds;<br>3. if no assignment claims exactly the produced set of d, validation fails;<br>4. if two different assignments i ≠ j both claim exactly the produced set of d, validation fails. | <pre>fun {N} V ↦<br>  (∀ (o : Out N) (d : List N) (i : ℕ),<br>      ExactAssignment o d i → (∀ (j : ℕ), ExactAssignment o d j → j = i) → V o d = true) ∧<br>    (∀ (o : Out N) (d : List N), (∀ (i : ℕ), ¬ExactAssignment o d i) → V o d = false) ∧<br>      ∀ (o : Out N) (d : List N) (i j : ℕ),<br>        i ≠ j → ExactAssignment o d i → ExactAssignment o d j → V o d = false</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A validator is the verdict function of one implementation: its inputs are the declared output spec and the tokens the completed action produced, as a deposit (a list of places with repetition); its output is success (true) or violation (false). The produced set is the places of the deposit (glossary: Produced set).
- When an action completes, an executor with output validation enabled, which is the default ([CONC-026]), shall validate its produced set against the transition's output spec. This sentence is the trigger and is not encoded: the block fixes the verdict of the check, not when it runs. An executor with the CONC-026 skip enabled runs no check, so the verdict constrains nothing there (CONC-026's subject). A completion that fails validation deposits no outcome and is not a firing (glossary: Fire); a transition with no output spec has nothing to validate; the timeout outcome (IO-013) does not complete the action and is not validated.
- Assignments are counted, not their claims: two assignments that claim the same set are two (Xor(A, A) with A produced has two exact assignments and fails). Out.branches enumerates one claim per assignment, in the claim rules of the bullet list; n-ary And and Xor are read as right-nested binary nodes, which changes neither the number of assignments, their claims nor their IO-016 order.
- The verdict function is total: the three clauses range over every output spec, those IO-012 rejects at build included. The sentence 'For a transition that builds, no two assignments claim the same set' is a consequence, not a clause: the glossary's WellFormedOut is the build-time constraint on an output spec, and on a spec satisfying it no two different assignments are exact for any deposit (theorem IO_015_wf_noAmbiguous), so under IO-015 the verdict is success exactly when some assignment is exact (theorem IO_015_wf_iff). The theorems use only its IO-012 conjunct, pairwise distinct branch sets.
- The set-of-places sentence is a consequence, not a clause: the three clauses fix the verdict from the number of exact assignments (theorem IO_015_determines), and that number reads only the produced set (theorem IO_015_setOnly). Deposits are compared per place (CORE-036): two deposits with the same count in every place, in any order, get the same verdict (theorem IO_015_perPlace).
- AC1 (conforming output → success) is the first clause, 'conforming' read as 'exactly one exact assignment'.
- AC2 (non-conforming output → failure event emitted) and AC3 (consumed input tokens are not restored on failure) are executor behaviour on a failed completion ('Validation failure is treated as a transition failure', EVT-007); the failure is not a firing of the reference semantics, which has no failed completion, so neither is encoded here.
- AC4 (zero exact assignments → violation; two or more → violation) is the second and third clauses.
- AC5 (Xor(And(A, B, C), And(A, B)) with A, B, C produced → success) is checked for every validator satisfying IO-015 (theorem IO_015_ac5).
- AC6 (output spec declared, action produces nothing → validation failure on every firing) is checked for every validator satisfying IO-015 and every output spec (theorem IO_015_ac6): every claim names at least one place, so none equals the empty produced set. That the built-in passthrough is rejected before execution is CORE-043's subject.
- AC7 (mid-action publication) fixes which tokens make up the deposit the validator sees (published places count) and that published tokens stay in the marking on a violation; both are executor behaviour outside the verdict function and are not encoded. With the published places in the deposit, the AC7 verdicts (And(A, B) with A, B produced → success; with A only → violation) are checked on the reference validator.
- AC8 (And is unordered) is not a separate clause: a claim is compared as a set, and permuting the children of an And permutes the assignments and the order of places within a claim. It is checked for the AC8 pair And(A, Xor(D, E)) / And(Xor(D, E), A) on every produced set drawn from A, D, E and one foreign place, the empty one included (theorem IO_015_ac8).
- AC9 (Xor(And(A, C), B) with B and C produced → violation) is checked for every validator satisfying IO-015 (theorem IO_015_ac9).
- AC10 (Xor(And(A, Xor(D, E)), A) with only A produced → success) is checked for every validator satisfying IO-015 (theorem IO_015_ac10).

**Evidence**

  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_015 ioRefValidate</pre>

- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_015_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_015): All three clauses are exercised by the reference validator: AC5 has exactly one exact assignment and succeeds; AC9 has none and fails; `Xor(A, A)` with A produced has two and fails. `Xor(A, A)` does not build and the AC5 spec does, so the build-time constraint of `IO_015_wf_noAmbiguous` is neither empty nor everything.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_015_firing"></a>
## IO-015: Output validation: a validated write is deposited

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_015_firing` · Kind **B** · Scope executors with output validation enabled (the default, CONC-026) · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `af6e82b5561b0880`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *If* exactly one assignment claims a set *equal* to the produced set, *then* validation *shall* succeed.<br><br>The comparison is over **sets of places**: a spec names places, not counts, so an action that deposits several tokens into one claimed place conforms. | 1. For every transition t with output spec o, every marking m at which t is enabled and every deposit d such that exactly one assignment of o claims exactly the produced set of d:<br>2. some firing of t from m deposits d. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (o : Out N),<br>    OutputSpec t = some o →<br>      ∀ (m : CountMarking N),<br>        Enabled t m →<br>          ∀ (d : List N),<br>            (∃ i, ExactAssignment o d i ∧ ∀ (j : ℕ), ExactAssignment o d j → j = i) →<br>              ∃ m', F t m d m'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The firing-level reading of the IO-015 sentences quoted: the block IO_015 fixes the validator's verdict; this one ties the verdict to firings. An action may write any tokens (glossary: Deposit), a completion that passes validation deposits what the action wrote, and one that fails is not a firing (glossary: Fire). So every write that validation accepts is the deposit of some firing. 'Conforms' is read as 'passes validation and is deposited', as IO-016 AC4 says of such a write ('the tokens are deposited regardless').
- Timing and priority play no part: whether the action completes within a Timeout budget is nondeterministic (IO-013), so a validated write is always one way a firing can end.
- CORE-036 compares deposits per place and asks only that the places a firing deposits in be the places of one output branch, each receiving one or more tokens, so it allows such a deposit: both blocks hold of the validated-write executor (theorem CORE_036_IO_015_firing_consistent).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_015_firing_validated` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✓ `IO_015_firing` holds of the validated-write firings (`refFireValidated`): a write with exactly one exact assignment passes the reference validator (`IO_015_reference`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_015_firing refFireValidated</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_015_firing_forbids_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✗ The reference semantics deposits exactly one token per claimed place, so the leaf `Place(0)` never deposits the validated write `[0, 0]`.
  <pre>¬IO_015_firing RefFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_015_firing_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): `IO_015_firing` is exercised: the reference validator accepts `[0, 0]` for `Place(0)`, the validated-write firings fire it from the empty marking, and it is not an output branch.
  <pre>ioRefValidate (Out.place 0) [0, 0] = true ∧<br>  (∃ m', refFireValidated (clashT (Out.place 0)) clashEmpty [0, 0] m') ∧<br>    ¬OutputBranch (clashT (Out.place 0)) [0, 0]</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_036_IO_015_firing_consistent` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✓ CORE-036 and `IO_015_firing` hold together: the validated-write firings satisfy both. They are the reference firings with more deposits, and every extra deposit marks exactly the places of one output branch, which is what CORE-036's outcome line asks of a firing that ends with its action.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N],<br>  CORE_036 refFireValidated ∧ IO_015_firing refFireValidated</pre>
